# EII build task queue

This is the ordered queue consumed by the build run.
Each task is a few focused units of work with a visible or executable result.
Tasks in slice S0 through slice S3 are deliberately fine-grained and each should fit one short agent assignment.
Tasks in slice S4 through slice S11 are coarser and are refined at the preceding slice gate.
The queue was re-planned on 2026-11-12 for [decision 0016](docs/decisions/0016-version-1-covers-the-whole-initiative.md) and [decision 0017](docs/decisions/0017-public-data-additions-and-public-splits.md): slices S1 to S3 lay the map and data foundation, slices S4 to S6 build the flagship location choice in full depth, slice S7 builds the other journeys and the three services, and slice S8 adds the four cases and the assistant across every module (PLN-23 in [14_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md](docs/14_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md)). Slice S0 is unchanged. The queue was extended the same day for [decision 0019](docs/decisions/0019-forecasts-estimates-scenarios-and-self-serve-sources.md): the five fetched self-serve sources and their tables in slice S2, the Public context cards in slice S3, the pooled hourly anchor, the weather profiles and the population-grid weights in slice S4, the forecasts, base rates, people present by pay band, site estimate, 3D view and provider switch in slice S6, and the two scenario cards in slice S7 (PLN-28). It was extended on 2026-11-13 for [decision 0023](docs/decisions/0023-ten-additions-and-the-data-rule.md): the national GIS centre's layers, property sales, bank credit, tenders, district cooling, the ten open-data families and building heights in slice S2 (S2-T19, S2-T20), all of Qatar in 3D with a clock, the map modes and the financial centre's firms by tower in slice S3 (S3-T16, S3-T17), talent available near this site and the best three sites with "how sure" in slice S6 (S6-T20, S6-T21), and asking aloud in slice S8 (S8-T10); its data rule amends the criteria of existing tasks by one line each (PLN-29). Its item 15 makes the product one for companies only, designed for three personas: S9-T04, the oversight screen, is withdrawn, slice S9 keeps the two company seats and the internal question log with no screen, and scene 7 of the guided story becomes the international entrant's headquarters evidence pack (PLN-30).

## Queue rules

- Under [decision 0055](docs/decisions/0055-mutation-testing-moves-to-after-go-live.md) items 1 to 4, no mutation campaign, kill pass or survivor-fix task runs before go live; unfinished mutation work is deferred to the receiving team after go live, if it approves. No task, train or slice gate waits for campaign closure, survivor disposal or a mutation-only follow-up item; the general open-item closure rule below excludes those deferred items. Completed campaign evidence stays recorded. Every other acceptance criterion, ordinary test, review and gate check remains required.
- One task uses one branch and one pull request. The only exception is an approved train of one slice under [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) rule 33 and [decision 0032](docs/decisions/0032-approved-tasks-of-one-slice-may-merge-as-a-train.md).
- Feature work is never committed directly to the base branch.
- The lead permits GitHub pushes strictly one at a time.
- The builder and first reviewer must come from different makers.
- Every task brief starts with the stated builder persona and reviewer persona.
- A task is done only when it is built, verified, reviewed, merged and green in CI.
- A reviewer returns only `APPROVED` or `REJECTED`; a rejection includes exact fixes. After round 1 only the kinds of finding of REV-39 reject; other valid points are proposed open items ([decision 0015](docs/decisions/0015-review-rounds-and-the-scope-of-repository-checks.md)).
- Acceptance evidence records the command or check, result, full commit hash and review verdict.
- Files outside the task's scope are read-only.
- Parallel tasks must have disjoint writable scopes; otherwise the lead serializes them.
- A task whose inputs already exist is built at once, in its own worktree, whatever its slice (the owner's decision of 2026-11-14). It is ready when each task on its Depends line is merged, or is built with the lead's checks green on a local commit it can stand on. A slice gate that has not passed holds back only the task's merge, not its building. A built task is committed locally and waits its turn: pushes and merges are one at a time, in dependency order; hosted checks may overlap under decision 0039, the critical path first, and the gate order of merges is unchanged. The review by another maker may run on the local commit. Before its turn the task takes `main`; if `main` changed a file it depends on, every check is rerun. Nothing in the checks changes: disjoint writable scopes, builder and reviewer from different makers, the lead's checks, the ledger, the head checks, the required hosted checks and the review. For a train, dependencies may merge together and the joined head takes the checks under [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) rule 33 and [decision 0032](docs/decisions/0032-approved-tasks-of-one-slice-may-merge-as-a-train.md).
- Each slice ends with a follow-up task before its gate that closes every proposed open item of the slice's reviews (REV-41); it is the last task of each slice below. Proposed open items go where [decision 0018](docs/decisions/0018-priorities-and-amendments-to-decision-0015.md) item 8 sends them: an item from a plan review goes into that task's build brief; an item raised by the review of a slice's follow-up task, or by a gate re-review, goes to the next slice's follow-up task, and in the last slice into the final report; the items of an approved pull request are entered by the next pull request's ledger commit, never on the approved head.
- Under [decision 0064](docs/decisions/0064-the-narrowing-rows-decided.md) item 45, a plan item left as an open item in place of the thing it owes closes in its own task before that task's gate, and any new narrowing of that kind goes to the owner first. The narrowing table names: the unproven criteria of S2-T01, S2-T04, S3-T16 and S4-T14 to S4-T16; the work merged with open items in S3-T03 and S3-T12; the cut scopes of the routes of S3-T08 and the as-of reading of S2-T27; the lineage identifier; the zone columns; the full employment feed (S4-T02); the four placeholder commands (OI-84); and the F5 calibration values, which are to confirm (OI-313). No criterion is ticked by this rule.
- The findings of a rejected gate review are fixed in a gate-fix task of that slice, which has its own entry in this file, its own branch, its own pull request and a full independent review, and is listed after the slice's follow-up task. A gate-fix task takes the next free number of its slice. Its branch and pull request may join an approved train of the same slice only under [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) rule 33 and [decision 0032](docs/decisions/0032-approved-tasks-of-one-slice-may-merge-as-a-train.md).
- Every build brief makes the builder break each condition of the code it writes, add a test for any break that nothing catches, and list the breaks in its return ([decision 0018](docs/decisions/0018-priorities-and-amendments-to-decision-0015.md) item 11). The break pass is short: after each break only the test files that cover the broken code run, the pass aims at about 15 minutes, and the full suite runs once at the end.
- A lead review with several internal lenses is used only for the calculation layer, the privacy engine and the release build. The difficulty `hard` does not decide it.
- Security beyond a task's acceptance criterion is built only in its simplest form. Hardening beyond that is never a verdict finding; it moves out of the plan into [17_DEPLOYMENT_HARDENING.md](docs/17_DEPLOYMENT_HARDENING.md), with its origin, at each plan's one refresh ([decision 0018](docs/decisions/0018-priorities-and-amendments-to-decision-0015.md) items 2 to 4).
- All tasks start with status `queued`.
- Requirement references below are to IDs defined in the numbered documentation pack; this file defines no requirement IDs.
- A task ID is never reused or renumbered. A task added by a re-plan takes the next free number of its slice and is listed where it runs, so numbers inside a slice need not be in order; a task split from another names it.
- Where a requirement added for decision 0016, decision 0017 or decision 0019 has no ID yet, a task names its document and section; the lead replaces that reference with the ID before the task becomes active (PLN-26).

Decision 0035 supersedes any visible-origin requirement in the existing task criteria below: check absence from audience output and presence of explicit backend markers instead, without changing any method, privacy or security assertion. S3-T18 is the map-base removal, S3-T19 builds the standing script, and later removal tasks reuse it. Backend marker implementation belongs to the existing contract/conformance task S4-T01, the release manifest/sanad task S5-T04 and the application-schema task S9-T01: each covers its generated records, tables, files or releases and database/ledger metadata, with generator name and version and a planted missing-marker failure. Slices S4 and S5 have no audience-mark removal task because their outputs are backend artefacts. Each new task runs before its slice gate and review follow-up; no already-recorded completion is changed.

Under [decision 0039](docs/decisions/0039-the-next-train-may-be-checked-while-the-previous-one-merges.md), the next pull request may open during the previous hosted run; its checks, train confirmation and lead ledger may overlap. After the previous merge, move the head to new main: a records-only move preserves approval with the diff attached; a code or test move needs new confirmation. Rerun required hosted checks on the moved head while the lead tests the exact squash-merge result with a fresh seed. Merge only with green required checks on that head, another maker's APPROVED holding for it and the local merged-result test green. Head checks, gates, hard limit and serialized pushes remain unchanged.

## Difficulty and personas

`routine` guides the lead towards a fast or mid-tier model.
`hard` guides the lead towards a strong model, with the top model reserved for expensive-to-get-wrong work.

Persona abbreviations used below:

- Platform: senior platform engineer / principal platform and supply-chain reviewer.
- Data: senior data engineer / principal data quality and provenance reviewer.
- Simulation: senior simulation engineer / principal statistical simulation reviewer.
- Privacy: senior disclosure-control engineer / principal privacy and adversarial reviewer.
- Backend: senior backend and database engineer / principal backend security reviewer.
- Methods: senior applied economist and calculation engineer / principal economic-methods reviewer.
- Frontend: senior bilingual product engineer / principal design, accessibility and data-visualisation reviewer.
- AI: senior AI systems engineer / principal AI safety and evaluation reviewer.
- Operations: senior demonstration producer / principal operational-readiness reviewer.

## Decision sweeps

### D0022 — Real register companies with an attached synthetic world
- Goal: Sweep the documentation pack to accepted decision 0022, keeping public register-company fields distinct from the attached synthetic labour and residence records.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] documents 01 to 17, the glossary, task queue and indexes reflect the decision; [x] AGENTS.md and CLAUDE.md are byte-identical with the amended rules; [x] named synthetic firms are retired in favour of `register_company` and `register_company_list`; [x] unresolved implications remain explicit OPEN questions; [x] prior accepted decisions remain unchanged and the documentation and tree checks pass, as recorded in the D0022 task log of PROGRESS.md.
- Scope: documentation pack, repository guidance and task records only.
- Depends: accepted decision 0022.
- Requirements: decision 0022, items 1 to 8; AGENTS.md rules 3, 8, 10 and 24.
- Delivery: hard; personas Data and Privacy; status done (pull request 37, merged 2026-11-13).

## slice S0 — Foundation

slice S0 begins with the environment, repository structure and dependencies.

### S0-T01 — Repository scaffolding and tool configuration
- Goal: Create the pinned repository layout and root configuration without implementing product behaviour.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] every pinned top-level folder exists; [x] ignores exclude generated data and secrets; [x] the tree check passes.
- Scope: root configuration, `contracts/`, `core/`, `custodian/`, `product/`, `web/`, `evals/`, `gateway/`, `infra/`, `scripts/`, `data/`.
- Depends: none.
- Requirements: ARC-01, PLN-22, TST-163.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 1, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T02 — Python workspace and three packages
- Goal: Create one `uv` workspace containing `eii_core`, `eii_custodian` and `eii_product`.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] Python resolves all three packages; [x] each package imports in a clean environment; [x] workspace metadata names no forbidden dependency edge.
- Scope: root Python workspace files, `core/`, `custodian/`, `product/`.
- Depends: S0-T01.
- Requirements: ARC-01, BE-01, TST-60.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 4, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T03 — Web application skeleton
- Goal: Create the React, TypeScript and Vite application shell with no feature UI.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] development build starts; [x] production build succeeds; [x] no run-time asset uses a third-party host.
- Scope: `web/`, web package and TypeScript configuration.
- Depends: S0-T01.
- Requirements: FE-01, FE-05, FE-07, DES-24.
- Delivery: routine; personas Frontend; status done (pull request 3, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T04 — Lint, format and type checks
- Goal: Make Python and web linting, formatting and type checks reproducible.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] each check has one documented command; [x] planted violations fail; [x] clean scaffolding passes.
- Scope: lint, formatter and type-checker configuration; check scripts only.
- Depends: S0-T02, S0-T03.
- Requirements: PLN-07, TST-161, TST-162, FE-11.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 5, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T05 — Test runners
- Goal: Make `./eii test` run every network-free Python and web pull-request suite.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] suite counts are printed; [x] a failing fixture makes the command non-zero; [x] clean suites exit zero.
- Scope: test configuration, test bootstrap files, `scripts/` test runner.
- Depends: S0-T02, S0-T03.
- Requirements: TST-15, TST-01, PLN-14, FE-95.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 7, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T06 — Pre-commit hooks
- Goal: Run formatting, lint, documentation, secret and identifier checks before commits.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] hooks install from repository configuration; [x] generated forbidden tokens are caught; [x] a commit guard refuses any staged value equal to a non-empty local secret without printing the value; [x] clean staged files pass.
- Scope: pre-commit configuration and scanner test fixtures.
- Depends: S0-T04, S0-T05.
- Requirements: TST-141, TST-144, PLN-05.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 19, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T07 — Continuous integration
- Goal: Build CI for tests, documentation checks, secret scanning, identifier-pattern scanning and the hosted-runner sandbox proof.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] all check classes run on pull requests; [x] the sandbox test runs on the hosted runner and is never skipped; [x] planted failures block CI; [x] the foundation commit is green.
- Scope: CI workflow files and CI-only setup scripts.
- Depends: S0-T04, S0-T05, S0-T06.
- Requirements: TST-61, TST-62, TST-140, TST-141, TST-150.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 21, merged 2026-11-13).
### S0-T08 — Compose and store preflight
- Goal: Add the portable database service and probes that select a store without starting or stopping it.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] Compose defines the pinned database and PostGIS line; [x] probes time out as specified; [x] absent PostgreSQL selects embedded with a warning; [x] with `EII_RELEASE_DIR` unset, foundation preflight exits zero, reports `WARN not_configured` for release, load-receipt, embedded-reader and recorded-replay checks, and keeps every other check's normal severity; [x] `up` still refuses without a configured complete release.
- Scope: `infra/compose.yaml`, preflight probe modules and probe tests.
- Depends: S0-T01, S0-T02.
- Requirements: OPS-06, OPS-09, OPS-17, BE-87.
- Delivery: hard; personas Platform; status done (part A, pull request 10, and part B, pull request 22, merged 2026-11-12; part C, pull request 24, merged 2026-11-13).
### S0-T09 — `./eii` command skeleton and database commands
- Goal: Create every pinned command entry point, including `db-up` and `db-down`, with safe placeholder dispatch.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] usage lists every pinned command; [x] `preflight` only probes; [x] `db-up` and `db-down` alone start and stop the database; [x] `up` never starts it.
- Scope: root `eii` launcher and `scripts/commands/`.
- Depends: S0-T08.
- Requirements: OPS-26, OPS-27, OPS-28, ARC-96.
- Delivery: hard; personas Platform; status done (pull request 15, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T10 — Import-boundary test
- Goal: Enforce the three-plane imports before feature code exists.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] direct, local and dynamic forbidden imports fail; [x] package metadata edges are checked; [x] clean packages pass.
- Scope: boundary test module and planted fixtures only.
- Depends: S0-T02.
- Requirements: ARC-06, BE-01, TST-60.
- Delivery: hard; personas Backend; status done (pull requests 8 and 11, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T11 — Generator sandbox test
- Goal: Prove locally and on the hosted CI runner that the `build-world` and `build-release` sandbox profiles cannot read outside their allowed mounts or open the network.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] both prohibited operations fail under both profiles locally and on the hosted runner; [x] the read tooth succeeds outside; [x] CI never skips the test; [x] a runner restriction on unprivileged user namespaces is handled by a per-binary bubblewrap AppArmor profile or fails the job; [x] engine extensions are fetched before sandbox entry into a project-local folder mounted read-only and load with the network off.
- Scope: sandbox launcher, hosted-runner namespace setup, project-local engine-extension folder, sentinel generator and sandbox tests.
- Depends: S0-T02, S0-T05.
- Requirements: ARC-09, ARC-10, ARC-14, SYN-01, TST-61, TST-62.
- Delivery: hard; personas Platform; status done (pull request 14, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T12 — Version and retreat pins
- Goal: Pin supported toolchains and libraries with written retreat pins where the design requires them.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] lock files resolve; [x] exact and retreat pins are labelled; [x] unsupported version drift fails a check.
- Scope: lock files, version files and dependency constraints.
- Depends: S0-T02, S0-T03.
- Requirements: FE-03, TST-153, PLN-15, TST-43, BE-13.
- Delivery: hard; personas Platform; status done (pull requests 9 and 12, and the follow-up pull request 18, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T13 — New-library reference notes
- Goal: Give agents verified official-reference notes for libraries newer than most model training data.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] each named newer library has an official-documentation pointer; [x] no API behaviour is asserted from memory; [x] slice S1 confirmation fields exist.
- Scope: dependency reference notes under `docs/` assigned to this task only.
- Depends: S0-T12.
- Requirements: FE-02, PLN-10, PLN-18, TST-01, DES-25, DES-45.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull requests 13 and 16, merged 2026-11-12).
### S0-T14 — GitHub repository settings
- Goal: Enable secret scanning, push protection and branch protection under OI-13.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] each setting is queried after change; [x] evidence records the result; [x] no repository file or git setting is changed by the task.
- Scope: GitHub repository settings only; repository files are read-only.
- Depends: S0-T07.
- Requirements: TST-141, PLN-06, PLN-12.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 23, merged 2026-11-13).
### S0-T15 — Review follow-up for slice S0
- Goal: Close every proposed open item that the reviews of slice S0 entered in `OPEN_ITEMS.md`, before the slice S0 gate.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] each item of the slice is closed by a fix or by one line saying why it is not valid or is out of scope under REV-42; [x] the reviewer checks every closure; [x] no item is deferred out of this task; [x] no item of the slice is left open.
- Scope: the files the closures change, and `OPEN_ITEMS.md`.
- Depends: every other task of slice S0.
- Requirements: REV-41, TST-168.
- Delivery: routine; personas as the items require; status done (part A, pull request 20, merged 2026-11-12; part B, pull request 25, merged 2026-11-13).
### S0-T16 — Gate fix for slice S0
- Goal: Fix the findings of the first review of the slice S0 gate: bring preflight, the example environment file and the commit guard in line with the checks and variables that decision 0019 added to the pack.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] `./eii preflight` runs the seventeen checks of the preflight table, the traffic-provider-key and night-lights-key checks among them, and neither of the two prints a key or makes a call; [x] a test runs preflight with a known dummy value for each of the three keys and finds none in the output; [x] `.env.example` holds the eleven variable names with empty values; [x] the commit guard refuses a staged value equal to the local value of `TOMTOM_API_KEY` or of `EARTHDATA_API_KEY` without printing it; [x] the status of S0-T12 names its three pull requests.
- Scope: preflight probe modules and their tests, `.env.example` with the tree check's list of its names, the commit guard and its tests, the documents that name those lists, and this file.
- Depends: S0-T15; the first review of the slice S0 gate.
- Requirements: OPS-06, OPS-07, ARC-97, ARC-98, TST-141, TST-169.
- Delivery: routine; personas Platform; status done (pull request 27, merged 2026-11-13).

## slice S1 — Technical spike

### S1-T01 — Arabic glyph and label-order spike
- Goal: Prove self-hosted Arabic glyphs render with hexagons below labels.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] Arabic labels render from local assets; [x] hexagons do not cover labels; [x] visual evidence is recorded.
- Scope: `web/spikes/glyphs/`, local font and glyph spike assets.
- Depends: S0-T03, S0-T12.
- Requirements: FE-46, FE-56, FE-57, DES-24, DES-10.
- Delivery: hard; personas Frontend; status done (pull request 32, merged 2026-11-13).
### S1-T02 — Map load and frame-rate spike
- Goal: Measure the pinned hexagon and particle load in a native host browser.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] 110,000 hexagons render; [x] 50,000 particles render. The criterion on the native trace moved to task S1-T12 by a re-plan of 2026-11-13.
- Scope: `web/spikes/performance/` and performance scripts.
- Depends: S1-T01.
- Requirements: TST-132, FE-88, PLN-07.
- Delivery: hard; personas Frontend; status done (pull request 34, merged 2026-11-13).

- Acceptance criteria: [x] routing has no server; [x] the drive isochrone changes with the hour through a fixture speed table, by the time-of-day mechanism this spike chooses; [x] each shape is also kept exactly, for Public and Simulated layers; [x] the snapped output contains only whole frame cells; [x] deterministic fixtures pass.
- Scope: routing and area spike under `product/` and its fixtures.
- Depends: S0-T02.
- Requirements: ARC-88, BE-82, PRV-10, SYN-53.
- Delivery: hard; personas Backend; status done (pull request 33, merged 2026-11-13).
### S1-T04 — Tile range-request spike
- Goal: Serve one local tile artefact with correct byte-range behaviour.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] full request succeeds; [x] valid ranges return expected bytes; [x] invalid ranges fail safely.

- Requirements: OPS-30, FE-56, FE-58, TST-167.

### S1-T07 — Offline typed-decision gateway
- Goal: Build the provider-neutral gateway contract, `none`, recorded and configured providers, decision records, data policy and import boundary.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] default `none` opens no network; [x] forbidden data classes are blocked; [x] records use the closed schema; [x] serving packages cannot import the gateway.

- Goal: Assign one hourly origin-destination fixture to the routing graph with one fixed volume-delay rule, and animate the resulting link speeds through the day on the map.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] the assignment is tested code that reads only a synthetic fixture and repeats exactly on identical inputs; [x] one fixed volume-delay rule is used, with every parameter in configuration; [x] the hourly link speeds feed a drive-time area that changes with the hour; [x] the animated road layer carries the Simulated mark (audience mark withdrawn by decision 0035 items 1 and 6; the backend origin marker of item 3 applies); [x] any library the assignment needs gets its exact and retreat pins through S1-T08. ### S1-T13 — The performance page saves native traces and refuses a software or remote renderer at once
- Goal: Make the serve command of the performance spike store a posted trace, and make the page show its WebGL renderer as soon as it loads and refuse a software or remote renderer, or a capped frame rate, on screen before the orbit in the gate profile.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] a test starts the development server of the serve command and posts a trace, which is stored, and a malformed post is refused in JSON; [x] the page shows the unmasked renderer at load; [x] in the gate profile a software or remote renderer, and a frame rate capped below the gate's need, are refused on screen before the orbit, and nothing is posted, with one planted case each; [x] no count, budget, profile or threshold of the gate changes.
- Scope: `web/spikes/performance/` (the server, the page, the trace judgement, their tests and the spike's README).
- Depends: S1-T02.
- Requirements: TST-132, FE-88, PLN-07.
- Delivery: routine; personas Frontend; status done (pull request 35, merged 2026-11-13). Added by a re-plan of 2026-11-13: the owner's first native run could not be stored (the development server answered the trace's post with an empty 404) and ran through a remote session capped at 30 frames a second; S1-T12 records its traces with the fixed page.
### S1-T14 — Place-scoped exception for one name of the private list in the documentation check
- Goal: Let one name of the private forbidden-pattern list pass in the map attribution line and in its own source rows of document 04, and stay refused everywhere else, with or without the private list. The repository knows the name only by the SHA-256 of its case-folded text ([decision 0024](docs/decisions/0024-one-private-name-in-two-places.md); the owner's note of 2026-11-14 on the satellite imagery provider's name, ruling L-151).
- Acceptance criteria: [x] a listed name passes on a source row of document 04 section 2 whose first-cell ids are all listed for it and on the map attribution line, which is the one line of `web/src/stage/attribution.ts` (a leading byte-order mark ignored) that is `export const MAP_ATTRIBUTION`, optionally `: string`, ` = `, one template literal with no backquote, backslash or dollar sign or one quoted string with no backslash and no quote of its own kind, and a semicolon, at column 0, starting outside every comment, string, template and bracket; the file may hold other code and comments, but no second such line, no vertical tab, form feed, U+001C to U+001E, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029 or carriage return not followed by a line feed, no hashbang line, no slash outside comments, no template substitution, no unclosed comment, string or template and no unbalanced bracket, in any letter case (planted: the invented name in a 2.1 row, in a 2.2 row with two ids and on the attribution line, in upper case, in lower case and in a case-folded form, and with a byte-order mark, a type annotation, a quoted string, other code and comments in the attribution file; exit 0); [x] the same name anywhere else fails with the unchanged hidden-text line (planted: section-2 prose, a source-shaped row outside section 2, inside a fence, after a non-blank line or in a table header, another source's row, a first cell mixing a listed and an unlisted id, another document, another line of the attribution file, a split, indented, repeated, block-commented, braced, template-wrapped or escaped declaration, a declaration followed by a second statement, a file with a slash outside comments, a template substitution or a hashbang line at its start, another web file, the configuration and a comment of `scripts/check_docs.py`); [x] document 04 has no allowed line when it holds `<!--`, a line-break trap character, a fence, a line starting with `<` before the end of section 2, a setext underline in section 2, or when a fence before section 2 hides a fake heading (planted: a form feed, a line separator, a comment row, a fake section in a comment, a tilde line inside a backtick fence, a short fence inside a long one, a fence in a list item ended by a dedented line before a fence that hides the one heading, an HTML block, an indented heading, a setext heading, a fence closed by a line with a tab after its marks, an HTML block before the heading that holds a fence-looking line and a no-break space, an ideographic space or a unit separator alone on a line or after the heading, a row or a delimiter row (a line that CommonMark does not call blank)); [x] every other entry of the private list stays refused in both places, and no built-in pattern is ever excepted (planted: a second invented name that is not listed, another spelling of the listed name, and a listed run of eight digits each fail; a link whose target holds an absolute local path or an eight-digit run fails the built-in patterns; a pattern file saved with a byte-order mark keeps its first pattern); [x] at most one entry; a second name needs a new owner note and a reviewed change of this limit in `scripts/check_docs.py` (planted: two entries fail and lift both names); [x] the configuration holds only digests, reasons and source ids, and a missing or malformed configuration fails the check, with or without the private list, and lifts every exception (planted: one case for each detail of the closed list); [x] nothing reaches standard error and no error text is shown (planted: a pattern that compiles with a warning, a deeply nested configuration, a zero byte in a link target, a link target whose existence cannot be checked, output in ASCII with a non-ASCII path and an unexpected error each end in fixed lines with nothing on standard error); [x] check 8 refuses a listed name in identifier forms in every file git lists and in paths, without the private list, and hides the path of such a file in every FAIL line (planted: snake case, a setting name, camel case, a dotted host in a `.mjs` file, `.env.example`, a file path, the reason field, the copy of the check and a two-part name); [x] no test reads the private list (every child run drops the variable and points it at a temporary file outside the scanned tree); [x] the configuration is committed with no entry, the private list is unchanged, and no name is written in the repository, a brief or a record.
- Scope: `scripts/check_docs.py`; the new `scripts/check_docs_place_exceptions.toml` and `scripts/tests/test_check_docs_exceptions.py`; TST-140 and TST-263 in document 12, REV-25 in document 15, the Attribution paragraph of document 10 section "7. The basemap"; decision 0024 and its row in `docs/README.md`; this file. The provider's rows in document 04 and the attribution file come with the task that adds the imagery source and the map's credits, whose first commit is the one real entry of the configuration, made before its builder starts; its reviewer checks the digest against the name as written in its places. That task's source ids, release tables, layer ids, file and folder names, setting and key names and dependencies carry no form of the name (whole, shortened, initials or altered); the provider's host and tile address come from a setting read at run time and appear in no committed host list, content security policy or fixture, and the credit holds no address; document 04 section 4, documents 03 and 10, requirements, tests, fixtures, evidence, commit messages, branch names and pull-request text name the provider by role only; no logo or other image of the provider is committed; visual baselines of the map mask the credit unless the owner rules that a rendered credit is part of the place; its brief names who writes the name into the two places and gives this list to any builder who receives it. No check sees commit messages, branch names or pull-request text, and a pushed pull-request ref stays public.

**slice S1 gate.** Arabic labels render with self-hosted glyphs; hexagons remain under labels; 110,000 hexagons and 50,000 particles render at 60 frames per second; an isochrone is turned into grid cells; walk and drive isochrones at different hours of day, a free-drawn polygon and a radius are each kept as an exact shape and turned into whole grid cells; one hourly origin-destination fixture is assigned to the routing graph with one fixed volume-delay rule, and its link speeds animate on the map; tiles are served with range requests; PostgreSQL and DuckDB answer one parity query; and the sandbox works. Each item passes its named test. A technical alternative is accepted only if it keeps the same user-visible capability, passes the same assertion and is recorded as a decision. No fallback is permitted for sandbox enforcement, store parity or any security check. The rules configuration fixes `export_parameter_set` (default `A`) and the presence and flow parameters of PRV-59 before any world is built, and the number of monthly periods shown first is recorded.

## slice S2 — Public pack, frame, spine, places and investment sources

### S2-T01 — Public fetchers and source receipts
- Goal: Register every Public input required by any pack document, fetch only allow-listed sources as a visitor's browser receives them, at a visitor's pace, with no sign-in, paywall, robot check passed or back-end call with a key or token found in their pages, and store complete provenance receipts.
- Acceptance criteria: [x] only `./eii build-public` opens source connections, with four named exceptions outside it: the optional traffic provider of decision 0019 item 17, which only the API process contacts, only with `EII_TRAFFIC_PROVIDER=tomtom`, `TOMTOM_API_KEY` present and the presenter's offline control not set (OPS-70, S6-T19); the imagery provider, for satellite tiles and address search, which only the API process contacts through its own paths, only with `ESRI_API_KEY` present and the offline control not set, with no tile or geocoding result stored (OPS-73, S3-T03, S3-T16; TST-248, TST-249); the photorealistic 3D tile provider, which only the API process contacts on a same-origin route, only with `MAPTILE_API_KEY` present and the offline control not set, under the project-wide spend cap and view budget, with no tile stored (OPS-74, S3-T16; TST-255); neither the imagery provider's hosts nor the photorealistic 3D provider's hosts are on the build-public allow-list; tests and continuous integration call each provider only on hand-made fixtures (TST-248, TST-249, TST-255); [x] every required Public input is present in the registry, including citation-only metadata; [x] every fetched file has URL, publisher, licence, retrieval date and hash; [x] the allow-list holds both portal hosts, `www.data.qa` and `www.data.gov.qa`, and the investment-source hosts `fdiprojects.invest.qa` and `www.invest.qa`, the host `data.source.coop` of the places copy of `places_fsq`, and, for each other source of decision 0019, the host fixed by the task that builds its fetcher (S2-T14, S2-T16), added to PUB-03 by a reviewed registry change; [x] the registry holds a row for each of `ntl_blackmarble`, `worldpop_qat_agesex`, `weather_openmeteo`, `busy_besttime` and `places_fsq`, and no row for `traffic_tomtom_flow` and `traffic_tomtom_range`, which are listed in document 04 for their terms and attribution because nothing of them is fetched into the cache; [x] the registry records the host each file came from, and a portal dataset is identified by its slug whichever host served it; [x] no secret is printed, logged, committed or put into a work tree, a brief or a sandbox (rule 14); [x] nothing fetched is committed. Moved to task S2-T21, unticked and word for word, by a re-plan of 2026-11-14 (decision 0026 rewrote both sentences after this task was built and approved, and the registry loader does not yet follow them; open item OI-164): first, every visitor-readable source is used and shown in full whatever its reuse terms; each source records the publisher's terms in its own words with the date read and the basis of use in document 04; the registry records the stated open licence with `licence_basis: dataset` or portal terms with `portal_terms`, `provider_terms` for a provider's interface used under that provider's own terms (PUB-95 and PUB-97), and every other source with no open licence covering the use has `owner_decision` on the owner's decision for a personal, local project ([decision 0026](docs/decisions/0026-public-sources-used-in-full-locally.md)); no source needs a separate decision record; `busy_besttime` values stay in the uncommitted public cache, never in a test fixture, and the source stays out of the nightly live fetch; second, for every source the registry records the publisher's terms in its own words with the date read and the basis of use; changed terms update document 17, DHA-102, where permission or a licence is needed for real use, and never switch off a visitor-readable source or limit its release or display; unfetched sources, unconfirmed files, addresses or schemas, failed checks and sources that cannot be read as a visitor still remain absent with `NO_DATA` and their caveat, with the actual reason recorded.
- Scope: `custodian/publicdata/fetch/`, source registry and fetch tests.
- Depends: S1-T08.
- Requirements: PUB-01, PUB-03, PUB-09, PUB-11, PUB-19, TST-50, PUB-85, PUB-87, PUB-88.
- Delivery: hard; personas Data; status done (pull request 36, merged 2026-11-15). Two acceptance criteria that [decision 0026](docs/decisions/0026-public-sources-used-in-full-locally.md) rewrote after the task's approval are not met by its registry loader and are not claimed: by the re-plan of 2026-11-14 recorded in `PROGRESS.md` they are moved, unticked and word for word, to task S2-T21 with open item OI-164.

### S2-T11 — Portal additions of decision 0017
- Goal: Fetch, parse and register the 77 further portal datasets of decision 0017, handle their known traps, and build their Public tables under the Public split rule.
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] each of the 77 slugs has its registry row with publisher, licence, rows, coverage and latest period as document 04 records them, and the fetcher records them again at every fetch; [ ] each known trap that document 04 lists for these datasets has a parsing test, among them counts held as text, periods held as columns, municipality spelling variants mapped through `municipality_names.csv`, school years held as text, and the identifier columns of the residential unit sales dropped before the release with a deal count beside every price; [ ] museum venue names are normalised through a committed, reviewed crosswalk, and no value is edited by hand; [ ] official figures split by sex or nationality enter only Public tables, through their registry entry, on the source's own geography and categories; [ ] the schema tests reject sex and unauthorised gender or nationality on those contracts, protected tables and generator outputs; employment gender under decision 0037 is permitted, and admit only the movement split tables of DAT-169 and the nationality fields that DAT-141 permits (`nationality_group` on employment and establishment nationality on the register company); [ ] a Public figure split by sex or nationality is never used to segment worker, residence or company records and is never joined to one, is never combined with a protected figure, and no tabulated split is apportioned to a drawn area (the one case of whole cells of `pub_pop_agesex_grid` summed over an area is decision 0019 item 22, built in S3-T14); its one admitted use on simulated movement is SYN-89's cited aggregate anchors of the movement generator, summed over the source's own categories (DAT-141; decision 0023 items 12 and 16; TST-209); TST-209's movement-anchor case passes, and its worker-record case and its joining-key case each fail; [ ] every figure carries its source, its reference year or month and its retrieval date.
- Scope: portal source modules for the added datasets under `custodian/publicdata/`, the venue crosswalk, their registry rows and tests.
- Depends: S2-T01, S2-T02.
- Requirements: PUB-08, PUB-09, PUB-14, PUB-16, PUB-27, TST-50, PUB-85, PUB-89, PUB-90, PUB-91, TST-209.

### S2-T14 — Night-time radiance, population grid and weather history
- Goal: Fetch and register night-time radiance by month, the population grid by age and sex and the hourly weather history, and build their Public tables and cache file.
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] `./eii build-public` fetches `ntl_blackmarble` (NASA Black Marble VNP46A3, monthly) with `EARTHDATA_API_KEY` read from the environment only, aggregates it to the frame by whole cells and builds `pub_night_lights` (frame cell, month, radiance; whole pixels, none split), where a frame-cell value is the mean of the kept pixel values whose centre lies in the cell, fill values and pixels flagged poor are left out, no pixel is split, and `NO_DATA` is returned rather than zero where no value exists, with the MTH-109 fixture regression, and with NASA acknowledged as the source and the product DOI cited; [ ] `worldpop_qat_agesex` (100 m grids, CC BY 4.0) loads into `pub_pop_agesex_grid` (grid cell, age band, sex, people, year) with its source, year, licence and DOI, and enters only Public tables through its registry entry; [ ] `weather_openmeteo` is fetched into the public cache as a hashed file with the attribution the publisher asks for, and is a generator input and no release table; [ ] each registry row records publisher, licence or terms as read, retrieval date and hash, and the host of each fetcher is fixed by this task and added to PUB-03 by a reviewed registry change; [ ] each fetcher is tested only on hand-made fixtures in the documented response format, no response of a live service and no key is committed, printed or logged, and no test, gate or command needs the key or the network; [ ] what `./eii build-public` does when `EARTHDATA_API_KEY` is absent is fixed by this task and recorded in OI-103: it continues and leaves the night-lights layer out, either with a warning line that names the key and the layer left out or with no line, and it never fails for the absent key, and exit 6 stays for an unavailable network only; [ ] `ntl_blackmarble` is left out of the nightly live fetch; [ ] the schema tests reject sex and unauthorised gender or nationality on those contracts, protected tables and generator outputs; employment gender under decision 0037 is permitted, and admit only the movement split tables of DAT-169 and the nationality fields that DAT-141 permits (`nationality_group` on employment and establishment nationality on the register company), and nothing fetched is committed.
- Scope: night-lights, population-grid and weather fetchers under `custodian/publicdata/`, their registry rows, hand-made fixtures and tests.
- Depends: S2-T01, S2-T02.
- Requirements: PUB-01, PUB-03, PUB-04, PUB-19, TST-50, PUB-90, DAT-141, PUB-96, PUB-97, PUB-98, PUB-99, PUB-100.

### S2-T16 — Relative busyness of named venues
- Goal: Read the relative busyness by hour of the named venues of a build-time list from the provider's interface into `pub_venue_busyness` as Public facts of `place_public` subjects, inside `./eii build-public` only ([decision 0031](docs/decisions/0031-venue-busyness-from-a-provider-interface.md)).
- Acceptance criteria: [x] the project's own reader of the provider's interface fetches the values for the named venues of a build-time list, inside `./eii build-public` only, never at run time, never for a drawn area and never from a user's choice of site; [x] in a run it sends, for each venue, at most one request that creates the venue's forecast and, after it, at most one query of the stored forecast that reads the provider's update date, and never a second request of either kind for one venue, with a planted second request of each kind failing; [x] the reader takes the run's credit limit from the lead's shell, never from a committed file, and stops before a request that would pass it, sending nothing further in that run and recording the credits used in the receipt, with a planted request past the limit failing; [x] the two keys are read from the environment only, and only the lead's own shell runs a keyed fetch; where the provider takes a key as an address parameter, the address that carries it is never written to a log, receipt, error, cache name, fixture or file, and every failure message names a key-free form of the address (PUB-06), with a planted key in each of those places failing; [x] an answer whose shape differs from the documented shape fails the build with a message naming the source and the key-free address, and nothing is loaded from it; [x] `pub_venue_busyness` holds venue, day of week, hour, relative index 0 to 100, retrieval date and the provider's update date or null, and each value is a Public slot of a `place_public` subject labelled "BestTime relative busyness" with its retrieval date and the provider's update date, or "update date not stated" where the answer states none, and never called a forecast or foot traffic; [x] a value is a relative index and is never a count of people; only the venue's own hourly figures and their update date are taken, with a planted further field failing; [x] a venue for which the provider publishes no figure shows no figure, with that reason, and nothing is filled in from another venue; a venue counts only when the crosswalk of PUB-101 matches the provider's venue to the one asked for, the matched name compared in memory and never committed; [x] the registry row `busy_besttime` replaces `busy_gmaps`, with the provider as publisher, `licence_basis: provider_terms` and no decision record, `commit_allowed: false` and the provider's terms recorded truthfully as the licence text with the date read; an empty terms text is recorded as pending and fails nothing ([decision 0059](docs/decisions/0059-publisher-terms-are-a-record-not-a-gate.md)); the whole sentence of the terms page on the origin of the figures is copied into document 04 at the lead's terms read before the first fetch; the host joins the allow-list of PUB-03 by a reviewed registry change; [x] every place in the code and tests that holds `busy_gmaps` holds `busy_besttime`, with no other change of what it checks; [x] the values stay in the uncommitted public cache and never enter the repository, a test fixture, a brief or a document, and the reader is tested only on small invented answers of the provider's documented shape, with no key and no network; [x] the source is left out of the nightly live fetch, and the list of TST-52 is renamed and not widened; [ ] the lead's keyed run stays within the owner's monthly credit cap; the cap's number, the plan's name and price and every account detail stay out of the repository.
- Scope: the reader of the provider's interface under `custodian/publicdata/` and `custodian/src/eii_custodian/publicdata/`, its registry row, the renamed id in the registry's code and tests, invented answers and tests, and the terms sentence of document 04's row.
- Depends: S2-T01, S2-T06.
- Requirements: PUB-01, PUB-03, PUB-06, PUB-12, PUB-13, PUB-47, PUB-98, PUB-101, DAT-85, DAT-157, TST-52, TST-237.
- Amendment ([decision 0064](docs/decisions/0064-the-narrowing-rows-decided.md) item 8): [ ] the lead's capped keyed run is made from the lead's own shell inside the free credits the owner has already decided, and is then refreshed by a scheduled keyed run from the lead's own shell; the source stays out of the nightly live fetch, its values stay in the uncommitted cache, and each value is shown as relative busyness, never as a count of people (rules 4 and 24).
- Delivery: hard; personas Data; status in progress: offline busyness reader; the lead-only capped keyed run and account checks remain open; approved-head work in review in pull request https://github.com/albarami/economic-investment-intelligence/pull/82 as part of merge train 1 of slice S2. ### S2-T19 — The national GIS centre's layers, property sales, bank credit, tenders, district cooling and the ten open-data families
- Goal: Fetch and register inside `./eii build-public` the public layers of the national GIS centre's map service, the measures of the Justice Ministry's two sales bulletins, the banks' credit by economic activity, public tenders, district-cooling capacity and the ten open-data families of [decision 0023](docs/decisions/0023-ten-additions-and-the-data-rule.md) (items A3, A8 and A10), so that the data is collected before the design of its use.
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] every source of the task has its registry row with publisher, dataset or service, licence or documented basis of use and retrieval date and hash; every source records the publisher's terms in its own words with the date read and the basis of use; `licence_basis: owner_decision` on the owner's decision for a personal, local project applies wherever no open licence covers the use, except a provider's interface used under that provider's own terms with `provider_terms` (PUB-95 and PUB-97) ([decision 0026](docs/decisions/0026-public-sources-used-in-full-locally.md)); a planted registry row with no basis of use (`licence_basis`) is recorded with basis `pending`, stays in use and is refused for commit ([decision 0059](docs/decisions/0059-publisher-terms-are-a-record-not-a-gate.md)); a planted source whose terms are not yet recorded stays in use and is listed as 'terms to be read', with the date checked; planted fixtures with absent or prohibitive terms remain in use with those terms recorded truthfully in the registry; every visitor-readable source is used and shown in full whatever its reuse terms; [ ] the GIS centre's host `services.gisqatar.org.qa` and every other new host are added to the allow-list of PUB-03 by a reviewed registry change made by this task, and a planted request to a host off the list fails; [ ] each layer of the GIS centre is read with no sign-in, and a planted layer that answers with a sign-in or token demand is recorded as unavailable and loads nothing; [ ] `QARS` keeps only the zone, street and building numbers, the parcel number, the building subtype, the survey date and the point, `CadastrePlots` only the parcel number, area and dates, and a planted utility account number or internal editing field in a fixture fails the build and reaches no cache output or table; [ ] the imagery history is read through the service's export operation in web Mercator only for the fixed before-and-after views of a committed list, and a planted export request for a view off the list fails; [ ] the property type and use of both sales bulletins are read from the Arabic label and the usage field through a reviewed, committed mapping table, the English label is never used, and a planted unmapped label fails the build; [ ] transaction and parcel identifiers are dropped before the release, with a planted identifier column (PUB-14); [ ] the entry of the quarterly price series of card C41 in the forecast-series record of S2-T17 gets its period, frequency and number of past values here (PUB-103); [ ] no price, guide price or other figure is stored or labelled as a rent, a vacancy or a tenancy, no plot is stored as approved or available, and a planted rent label on a price or an availability field on a parcel fails (decision 0023 item 12; TST-247); [ ] the terms of the tender portal and of each of the ten open-data families are read and recorded in the publisher's own words with the date read, without making that reading a condition of fetching or showing; a planted prohibitive terms page is recorded truthfully in the registry while the visitor-readable source stays in use in version 1 (PUB-108); when a real source's terms are read and need a permission or licence for real use, that source is added to document 17's list, DHA-102; [ ] a source published by sex or nationality enters only as a Public split declared in its registry entry, and a planted sex column on a source without the declaration fails (PUB-90); [ ] each fetcher is tested only on hand-made fixtures in the documented response format, no response of a live service is committed, every derived file stays under `data/`, and nothing fetched is committed.

### S2-T20 — Building heights for all of Qatar
- Goal: Give every building of the basemap a height in the order of [decision 0023](docs/decisions/0023-ten-additions-and-the-data-rule.md) item A2 (the recorded height; the floor count times a stated storey height; the open heights of 3D-GloBFP; the WSF 3D 90 m cell mean) and measure the error of each fallback against the recorded heights.
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] the order is applied building by building and the source of each height is kept with it, and a planted building that holds both a recorded height and a 3D-GloBFP height keeps the recorded one; [ ] the storey height is one stated constant of the asset manifest (`ASSUMPTION` confirmed here), and a planted building with a floor count and no recorded height gets exactly that count times the constant; [ ] the error of each fallback against the recorded heights of the buildings that hold both is computed by tested code on a hand-made fixture whose arithmetic a reader can check, and is written to the asset manifest for "More evidence"; [ ] 3D-GloBFP is registered under CC BY 4.0 with the credit "Che et al. 2024", and the terms of WSF 3D are read and recorded, never as a condition of its use; [ ] a visitor-readable height source is used whatever its reuse terms; a planted registry row with a non-commercial licence stays in use with the terms recorded truthfully in the registry; when a real source's terms are read and need a permission or licence for real use, that source is added to document 17's list, DHA-102; [ ] heights are estimates and never a data value, and a planted extrusion of a building by a data value fails (FE-50, FE-52); [ ] both fetchers run inside `./eii build-public`, their hosts are added to PUB-03 by a reviewed registry change, they are tested on hand-made fixtures only, and nothing fetched is committed.
- Scope: height fetchers under `custodian/publicdata/`, the height step of the asset pipeline, their registry rows, manifest entries, fixtures and tests.
- Depends: S2-T01, S2-T02.
- Requirements: PUB-01, PUB-03, PUB-04, PUB-11, PUB-106, FE-50, FE-52.
- Amendment ([decision 0064](docs/decisions/0064-the-narrowing-rows-decided.md) item 25): [ ] the hand list of landmark heights is built, each height with its cited source; GlobalBuildingAtlas is measured against the recorded heights, its terms read as a record (decision 0059), and its measured error recorded for its place in the height order (OI-310); the heights step cannot be skipped, with a planted skip refused.
- Delivery: hard; personas Data; status in progress: building-height methods and reader mechanisms; complete live source and asset evidence remain open; approved-head work in review in pull request https://github.com/albarami/economic-investment-intelligence/pull/82 as part of merge train 1 of slice S2. ### S2-T27 — The public snapshot archive and the re-fetch schedule.
- Goal: every public fetch keeps a dated, hash-pinned snapshot that is never overwritten, and each source carries a recorded re-fetch schedule, because publishers overwrite their tables and a series' history cannot be rebuilt later.
- Scope: the fetch layer of `./eii build-public` (custodian/src/eii_custodian/publicdata/fetch/ and the shared helpers of S2-T25), the source registry rows, the receipts, the release build's reading of a pinned snapshot, documents 03, 04 and 12. Extend what exists (receipts, registry, the monthly register snapshots of document 04's register rows); never a parallel store.
- Acceptance:
  [ ] each fetched file is kept under a snapshot path keyed by source id, retrieval date and SHA-256; an existing snapshot is never overwritten or removed (planted overwrite refused);
  [ ] a snapshot index records source id, retrieval time, the publisher's stated version or modification date where given, byte size and SHA-256; every read verifies the hash (planted tamper refused);
  [ ] identical bytes on a re-fetch record the check date without a second copy; changed bytes make a new snapshot; a snapshot's retrieval time never changes, and the index records the time of every successful check separately;
  [ ] every registry row carries a closed re-fetch cadence (daily, weekly, monthly, quarterly, annual, as published, or none with a reason); the weekly sales bulletins are weekly; the families of decision 0045 item 4 carry EII's recorded schedule, which does not depend on a confirmed publisher frequency (daily at 03:00 Asia/Qatar for tender notices, event pages, incentive windows and investment-project pages; weekly for registers, GIS layers and supplier credentials; monthly for register snapshots and each Overture release; as published for a statistical series only where document 04 records the publisher's publication-driven schedule, every revision kept); document 04's Overture rows state the publisher's release-removal window with its public page and retrieval date, or say it is unconfirmed; an unknown cadence is refused (planted); a row of a scheduled family without its cadence is refused (planted); a row of a fixed daily, weekly or monthly family whose publisher frequency is unknown keeps the family's cadence (fixture); a statistical series without a recorded publication-driven schedule, and any other row with no fixed-schedule family and no recorded publisher frequency, takes none with `frequency_unconfirmed` and no family, and such a row that acquires as published or a period is refused (planted, including an unconfirmed statistical series);
  [ ] an offline "due" listing derives from the cadence and the latest successful check recorded in the index which sources are due, with no network (planted clock in the test); an unchanged-byte re-fetch resets due without creating or altering a snapshot (fixture);
  [ ] the release-side readers that exist read a named snapshot by its hash, with no latest-file fallback: the frame (`frame/live.py` and every caller of its cached read), the series selected source, the trade manifests, the review cache fixture (`review/cache_fixture.py`, its cached read and its cached write), `review/activity.py`, `review/graph.py`, the data-test cache (`custodian/data_tests/cache.py`) and the spine triage reader (`scripts/spine_triage_io.py`); each of these paths has its own older-of-two selection test and its own planted tamper refusal (lead ruling L-697 item 1);
  [ ] the commit flags and decision 0034 hold: no value of a source without an open licence enters the repository; snapshots stay in the uncommitted cache;
  [ ] no network or key in any automated test; failures name the source and reason without secrets.
- Depends: S2-T01, S2-T25 (shared fetch helpers).
- Requirements: PUB-01 to PUB-16 of document 04 (search each); AGENTS.md rules 5, 7, 13, 19 and 37.
- Amendment ([decision 0064](docs/decisions/0064-the-narrowing-rows-decided.md) item 24): [ ] each of the 13 citation-only registry rows (twelve reference pages and the national GIS locator) is archived as a dated, hashed snapshot, each host added by a reviewed registry change (rule 5).
- Amendment ([decision 0064](docs/decisions/0064-the-narrowing-rows-decided.md) item 51): [ ] the snapshot archive is copied to a drive the owner holds, with no network integration, and the copy is checked by hash; this answers the open question below.
- Delivery: Deep (it pins hashes of released inputs); personas: principal data-platform engineer for provenance and immutable archives (builder); strict provenance and security reviewer (reviewer); status in progress: immutable snapshots, reviewed hash reads and cadence mechanisms; evidence of the original reviews must be attached; approved-head work in review in pull request https://github.com/albarami/economic-investment-intelligence/pull/82 as part of merge train 1 of slice S2. Unticked criteria remain open; no merge or slice gate is claimed.
Open question to settle in your plan with options: where the archive persists so that it survives the loss of the build machine (the cache folder is ignored by git), within AGENTS.md rules 13 and 14 and decision 0034. Answered by [decision 0064](docs/decisions/0064-the-narrowing-rows-decided.md) item 51: a copy on a drive the owner holds, with no network integration.

### S2-T28 - The public sources the advisor unblocked.
- Goal: the owner's standing direction is that every public source is fetched (decision 0023 item 12; decision 0026,
  used in full locally). Twelve registry rows still carry a skip reason that the advisor's reading now resolves. Lift
  each, with the facts it needs, so that `./eii build-public` fetches it through the normal seam with a receipt and a
  hash. The accident row is the exception: its year-partition plan, receipt and refusals are built and tested offline,
  and its activation is S2-T29's through S2-T25's shared bounded reader (lead ruling L-707 item K1).
- The twelve rows and what the advisor read (publisher; licence; record count; fields; modified):
  wage_sector: National Planning Council; CC BY; 98; year, sector, gender, paid_employment_workers,
    monthly_average_wage_qr; 2026-07-09.
  wage_bands_priv: Ministry of Labour; CC BY; 71; year, wage_group, number_of_occupations, percentage; 2026-09-26.
  work_hours_act: National Planning Council; CC BY; 266; year, gender, economic_activity, employed_persons,
    average_work_hours.
  cpi_group: National Planning Council; CC BY; 936; year, month, group_code, group, cpi_index; 2026-08-02.
  zone_population: National Planning Council; CC BY; 552; year, number_of_zone, zone, population, area_in_km,
    population_density_km2.
  census_hh_type_2020: National Planning Council; CC BY; 480; year, place_of_residency_on_census_night,
    type_of_household, gender, value.
  accident: Ministry of Interior; CC BY; 1,000,500 records (road accidents with zone, street, time, weather, road
    status and type, classification, nature, reason, severity, deaths); 2025-07-29. Fetched in bounded pages and kept
    as a table, never as map points.
  bnd_muni_2025: 8 records with geo_shape (the municipality polygons).
  places_ovt and bldg_ovt: the release bucket answers anonymously over plain HTTPS; a listing request of the form
    `https://overturemaps-us-west-2.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=release/2026-10-21.0/theme=places/type=place/`
    (and `theme=buildings/type=building/`) returns the object list. The parent rows check that listing against the
    registered part rows, which keep their existing whole-file fetch (lead ruling L-701 item G4); never a byte-range
    read and never a second Overture reader.
  worldpop_qat_2020: the registry URL is a folder; the file is
    `https://data.worldpop.org/GIS/Population/Global_2015_2030/R2025A/2020/QAT/v1/100m/constrained/qat_pop_2020_CN_100m_R2025A_v1.tif`
    (about 1.6 MB).
  census_2020_workbook: the archived URL returns an HTML frame; the raw file is the same archive address with `id_`
    after the timestamp:
    `https://web.archive.org/web/20240422094401id_/https://www.psa.gov.qa/Style%20Library/PSA/CensusDetailedResults/documents/Qatar_Census_2022_Final_Results.xlsx`
    (about 5.7 MB, an Office Open XML workbook). Its file name says 2022 while the row's title says Census 2020
    results: keep the row's identity, record the file name as found, and list the question for the lead.
- Acceptance:
  [x] each of the twelve rows has its resolved skip reason removed (the accident row then carries
      `partition_byte_bound_unconfirmed` until S2-T29) and carries publisher, licence id and basis, URL, fetch
      host, extension and, for the portal datasets, its columns as read; a row whose licence basis is CC BY says
      `dataset` with the licence's public address, and the decision record field follows the registry rule; the
      registry reader accepts the file and its existing tests stay green;
  [x] row-count bounds follow exactly the convention the already-active portal rows use (read it; never invent a
      tolerance); where no convention fits, the bounds stay empty and the return says why;
  [x] every row split by sex (wage_sector, work_hours_act, census_hh_type_2020 and any other the fields show) carries
      the `public_splits` value the registry uses for that split, and AGENTS.md rule 10 holds whole: such a figure is
      a Public fact in the source's own categories on its own geography, never joined to a synthetic record, never
      used to segment worker, residence or company records, never combined with a protected figure; reuse the
      existing guard and add a planted violation for a new row if the guard does not already cover it;
  [x] the accident dataset's bounded-page fetch is planned offline with one receipt that names every page and its hash
      and a total that must equal the published count, or the fetch is refused (planted short page, planted duplicate
      page); it is not activated in this task;
  [x] Overture places and buildings are discovered from the HTTPS listing and fetched by the existing part-row path,

      refused; planted off-host redirect refused);
  [x] the WorldPop and census rows fetch their direct files; the census fetch refuses an HTML body where a workbook is

  [x] the five rows owned by S2-T14, S2-T15 and S2-T16 keep their skip reasons; no other row changes;
  [x] commit flags stay false; decision 0034 holds (no value of a source without an open licence enters the

  [x] no network or key in any automated test; invented responses only; failures name the source and reason without

reviewed source and redirect hosts join the allow-list, and a planted off-host request or missing receipt fails, and a planted missing basis is recorded with basis `pending`, stays in use and is refused for commit ([decision 0059](docs/decisions/0059-publisher-terms-are-a-record-not-a-gate.md)); a planted unknown cadence or missing scheduled-family cadence fails; [ ] TLS verification remains enabled everywhere, with a planted disabled-verification setting refused; for the leaf-only chains of Monaqasat and the education ministry, host-scoped SHA-256 pins identify each issuing intermediate certificate, another-maker security review covers the pin contract, and planted wrong-pin, other-host and unpinned-chain cases fail without fetching; regression values that the method cards could not pin are produced, checked against the source and written into the cards; no file without a stated licence is committed; every added portal dataset and both investment sources have registry rows, with the host of each file and, for every source, the publisher's terms in its own words with the date read and the basis of use; no fetched investment page, record or project is committed, and the parsers pass on synthetic pages of the same shape; the visitor-readable tender portal or its absence for an access or technical reason, never its terms, the apportioning weight for drawn shapes and the page structure of the investment sources are recorded (PLN-25); every source of decision 0019 has its registry row with publisher, licence or terms and retrieval date, every fetched file has its hash, nothing fetched is committed, the relative-busyness source is registered on the provider's terms (`provider_terms`, [decision 0031](docs/decisions/0031-venue-busyness-from-a-provider-interface.md)) and is left out of the nightly live fetch, the live traffic provider has no registry row and no cached file, and the fetchers pass on hand-made fixtures with no key and no network; and the release of the places copy used, with its date, and the Public source of each series that card C30 forecasts, or that it has none, are recorded (PLN-25); and every source of [decision 0023](docs/decisions/0023-ten-additions-and-the-data-rule.md), the ten open-data families of item A10 among them, has its registry row with its publisher, licence or basis of use, terms read and retrieval date; terms never switch off a visitor-readable source or limit its release or display; a planted registry row with no basis of use (`licence_basis`) is recorded with basis `pending`, stays in use and is refused for commit ([decision 0059](docs/decisions/0059-publisher-terms-are-a-record-not-a-gate.md)); a planted source whose terms are not yet recorded stays in use and is listed as 'terms to be read', with the date checked; planted fixtures with absent or prohibitive terms remain in use with those terms recorded truthfully in the registry; no field the decision says is never taken is stored, and every building has a height with its source and the measured error of each fallback (S2-T19, S2-T20). The release reads every public input through S2-T27's reviewed snapshot hash with no latest-file fallback, every price carries that snapshot's dated "as of", and the offline due listing counts from each source's latest successful check, applies EII's fixed daily, weekly and monthly family schedules whether or not the publisher's frequency is confirmed, and preserves an unconfirmed frequency, including that of a statistical series without a recorded publication-driven schedule, as `none` with `frequency_unconfirmed`. This is slice S2 acquisition like S2-T19 and S2-T29, explicitly after its gate; no new periodic crawl or release card implementation.

- Depends: slice S2 gate approved; S2-T19, S2-T27, S2-T29. Excluded from S2-T10's blanket dependency; no earlier slice S2 task depends on this enrichment.

## slice S3 — Living map and Public-only answer

### S3-T01 — Store interface and portable query registry
- Goal: Implement named portable SQL and normalized results for the first Public tools.
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] tools cannot supply SQL; [ ] each registered tool has parity; [ ] the portability lint rejects forbidden SQL.
- Scope: `product/eii_product/store/`, named SQL and store tests.
- Depends: S2-T08.
- Requirements: BE-07, BE-26, TST-40.
- Delivery: hard; personas Backend; status queued.
### S3-T02 — Public release load in two stores
- Goal: Build, load and verify a `release_kind: public_only` release in PostgreSQL and the embedded reader.
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] the manifest contains no Protected or Simulated table and omits `window_key`, `protected_sha256`, `parameter_set`, `world_seed`, `scenario_sha256` and `contracts`; [ ] manifest-only loading succeeds in both stores; [ ] the Public tables of decisions 0017 and 0019, `fdi_projects` and the agency's records among them (`pub_venue_busyness`, `pub_night_lights`, `pub_pop_agesex_grid` and `pub_place_fsq` included), load in both stores with parity; [ ] the loader-written `load_receipt.json` is not a manifest artefact and has `state: complete` and the matching manifest `release_id`; [ ] an extra table loads nowhere; [ ] both stores expose the same release identity; [ ] configured preflight passes; [ ] freeze and export-register entry are refused for this release kind.
- Scope: loader modules, Public release fixture and loader tests.
- Depends: S3-T01.
- Requirements: BE-44, BE-46, DAT-70, DAT-84, TST-63.
- Delivery: hard; personas Backend; status queued.

### S3-T12 — The living map's Public layers
- Goal: Draw the map of Qatar with real geography and roads, the real business points, the FDI projects and Public statistics on their official geography, each layer with its source chain and backend origin marker, never an audience origin mark (decision 0035 items 1 and 3).
- Acceptance criteria: [ ] real business points come only from the public places data and public listings that document 04 registers, `pub_place` points above its display threshold, and carry the "open listings are incomplete" note; [ ] FDI projects are drawn at the geography the source publishes and never as a lookup of a firm; FDI projects are shown in full, with every field available on cards, lists and the map; each card, list and map legend carries collapsed credits naming the agency as publisher, the data gatherer as the page names it, the date read and a link to the dashboard, with the page's caveat that the data "includes estimates of publicly announced projects" and "does not capture all investment projects"; capital and jobs are labelled as announced estimates, never added to measured figures or shown beside them without that label; [ ] a Public statistic is drawn on its official geography, and a value apportioned to a drawn shape is labelled Inferred with its method and geography; [ ] Public figures split by sex or nationality (official figures, and the published grid of S3-T14) appear only as Public facts with source and year, in the source's own categories, and a tabulated split stays on its own geography; [ ] night-time radiance is drawn as a Public layer by area and month from `pub_night_lights`, and the Public places layer includes `pub_place_fsq`, shown with the date of the release used; [ ] no Synthetic or Simulated value, and no estimate, share or score, is attached to a real named place, business, building or FDI project; [ ] every layer opens its source chain with its retrieval date; [ ] at most one hero layer is visible.
- Scope: map layer registry, Public layer components and their tests under `web/`.
- Depends: S3-T02, S3-T09, S2-T06, S2-T12, S2-T14, S2-T19, S2-T28.
- Amendment ([decision 0023](docs/decisions/0023-ten-additions-and-the-data-rule.md) items A3 and 12): [ ] the national GIS centre's layers of S2-T19 are Public layers of the map, each on its own published geography with the centre's source line while it is on: the 2026 guide land prices by district, never called a deal price or a rent; rail lines with planned stations and protection zones, a planned station never shown as operating; health centres and their catchments; landmarks; land use and zoning, never shown as an approval, a permit or availability; address points, land parcels and commercial points; and the official district and zone boundaries; no Synthetic, Simulated or protected figure, and no estimate, share or score, is attached to any of their features, and a planted station shown as operating and a planted parcel labelled approved, available or for sale each fail (BRF-98, BRF-107, PUB-105, PUB-110, DAT-168, FE-153, DES-212).
- Amendment ([decision 0047](docs/decisions/0047-eiis-own-decision-backlog-and-acceptance-targets.md) item A29): [ ] from S2-T28's dated accident table, show accidents only on the geography and period the source supports beside the existing planned-rail lines and protection zones and S6-T13 access result; a missing supported geography returns `NO_DATA`, and a planted accident point map, cross-geography allocation, operating-rail label or unsourced safety verdict fails.
- Requirements: FE-24, FE-47, FE-48, FE-49, FE-60, DES-54, PUB-19, PUB-29, BRF-64, FE-103, FE-116, FE-125, MTH-46, MTH-121, PRV-122.
- Delivery: hard; personas Frontend; status queued.
